# Upstream edits — marker status and contribution candidates

- Scarlet and Violet 4.0.0 and Kakarot 1.70 add game profiles and generated shader tables. The PC splitter accepts build-specific regular variation pairs, and the packer loads optional shader keys once per split. The host adds elapsed integer timing rows and offline Bluetooth replies.

Generated by hand during the P0 marker-hygiene pass (2026-11-12), cross-checking
`git log --author=igawa6 --format= --name-only | sort -u` against `git log --author=igawa6 -p -- <file>`
for each upstream (non-Peanuts-owned, non-new) file. Peanuts-owned paths (`dm/peanuts/**`,
`android/app/src/main/java/dev/peanuts/**`, `android/app/src/peanuts/**`, `peanuts/**`, `PEANUTS.md`)
and files we added from scratch (`dm/gfx/tools/split_pool.py`, `dm/gfx/tools/ue_ondevice.py`,
confirmed with `git log --diff-filter=A` + `git cat-file -e upstream/android:<path>`) are out of scope.

## Round 2 (2026-11-12) — hunk-level re-check

A review simulated `upstream-update.sh`'s coming `--check` mode: every
added/changed line block vs `git merge-base HEAD upstream/android` must carry a `PEANUTS` marker
within the hunk or on the line directly above it — a header comment several lines above a later
hunk in the same file does **not** satisfy a later hunk. Round 1's file-header-only markers on
`shader_split.py`, `vka_tex.c`, `vka_exec.c`, `hle_audren.c` and `hle_fpslock.c` left 24 hunks
failing. Re-ran the check as `git diff -U1 <merge-base> -- <file>`, split into `@@`-hunks, and added
one trailing or line-above `PEANUTS`/`PEANUTS(upstream-candidate)` marker per still-unmarked hunk
(comment-only edits, code semantics unchanged — `py_compile` and the importer's cmake build both
still pass clean).

**Per-file hunks vs markers (round 2 close-out):**

| File | Hunks | Marked before round 2 | Marked after round 2 |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dm/gfx/tools/shader_split.py` | 14 | 7 | 14 |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_tex.c` | 12 | 3 | 12 |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_exec.c` | 3 | 1 | 3 |
| `dm/host/hle_audren.c` | 9 | 4 | 9 |
| `dm/host/hle_fpslock.c` | 5 | 4 | 5 |

All five files now have a marker in/above every hunk vs the merge-base. No shader-digest exception
file was touched this round either (`vk_android_build.py`, `vk_android_same.tsv` untouched, still
logged above).

**`dm/gfx/tools/split_pool.py` is a Peanuts-owned new file, not an upstream edit** (confirmed again
via `git log --diff-filter=A` + `git cat-file -e upstream/android:dm/gfx/tools/split_pool.py` → does
not exist upstream). The critic's simulated check flagged it as "1/1 unmarked" because it lives
under `dm/gfx/tools/` (an upstream directory) and the script's `is_peanuts_owned` exclusion list
does not yet cover that path — **this is a script bug, not a missing marker**, and per the
coordinator's instruction I did not add a marker to it. Package D (adding `--check` /
`is_peanuts_owned`) should list `dm/gfx/tools/split_pool.py` (and `dm/gfx/tools/ue_ondevice.py`,
already correctly excluded by file-ownership, not by path) as Peanuts-owned exclusions.

## Shader-chain exceptions (not edited, by instruction)

These files' bytes feed `DMSH_VK_CHAIN` (`dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_chain.py` FILES list) or
`DMSH_VK_CHAIN`/`DMSH_VK_ABI` directly (`dm/gfx/nvn/dmsh_chain_vk.h`); any edit — including adding
a comment — forces re-translating every installed game's shader library on the next build. Per the
brief, these are listed here rather than marked:

| File | Hunk summary (from `git log --author=igawa6 -p`) | Why unmarked | Upstream candidate |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dm/gfx/tools/vk_android_build.py` | `vk_android_build.py` changes referenced across several Peanuts commits (worker pool, zstd level plumbing read by `PackService`/`ue_ondevice.py`'s callers) | feeds `DMSH_VK_CHAIN` | Yes — the worker-pool and zstd-level changes are general perf wins (per review), not Peanuts-specific; should go upstream as a PR so this cost stops recurring on every sync |
| `dm/gfx/tools/vk_android_same.tsv` | two added rows mapping our tool digests onto the current chain version | feeds `DMSH_VK_CHAIN` (comparison table, byte-sensitive) | No — these rows are proof-of-no-change evidence for *our* specific chain state; they need to be re-proven against each new upstream chain version, not sent upstream as-is |
| `dm/gfx/tools/vk_android_spv.py` | `_remap_operands`: OpImageWrite and OpAtomicStore (instructions with no result id) get their own branches, so a promoted local's load is replaced in the image write's coordinate too (2026-11-14, OCTOPATH TRAVELER 0, issue #9: 2,142 of its 2,521 compute shaders failed spirv-val with "ID has not been defined"); `OP_ATOMIC_STORE` constant | feeds `DMSH_VK_CHAIN`; the change moves the chain version (`dmsh_chain_vk.h` 4b1886941d10 → 4ccbdc391b15) | Yes — a plain correctness fix in a translator post-pass; it changes the output only of modules that failed validation before |

| `dm/gfx/tools/vk_android_spv.py` (2026-11-14, second edit) | `buffer_textures_split`: a buffer texture the translator declares as a combined image and sampler of Dim Buffer becomes a plain sampled Buffer image (UNIFORM_TEXEL_BUFFER, no sampler); `OP_IMAGE` constant (OCTOPATH TRAVELER 0: 6,749 compute, 2,090 vertex and 74 pixel shaders' bindings read as unbound) | feeds `DMSH_VK_CHAIN` | Yes — a general fix: Vulkan has no combined descriptor of a texel buffer |
| `dm/gfx/tools/vk_android_build.py` (2026-11-14, second edit) | calls `buffer_textures_split` before reflection; the kinds `TEX_DYN` / `SAMP_DYN` for Ryujinx's `<stage>_tb_cb0_40000000` / `_s_cb0_40000000` fallback arrays (profile key `tex_dyn_slots`) | feeds `DMSH_VK_CHAIN` | Yes, with the host side (vka_bind.c) |
| `dm/gfx/nvn/dmsh_abi_vk.h` | `DMSH_VK_KIND_TEX_DYN` (23) and `DMSH_VK_KIND_SAMP_DYN` (24) | feeds `DMSH_VK_CHAIN` and `DMSH_VK_ABI` | Yes, with the two edits above |
| `dm/gfx/tools/vk_android_spv.py` (2026-11-21) | `dual_source`: a float `vec4` output 1 (`Location 0`, `Index 1`) is written through a Private shadow variable that is copied whole to the new Output variable before each return, instead of per component (FINAL FANTASY XII THE ZODIAC AGE: Qualcomm 512.676 refused 25 of 94 pipelines with `VK_ERROR_INITIALIZATION_FAILED`; Turnip accepts both forms) | feeds `DMSH_VK_CHAIN`; the change moves it (`dmsh_chain_vk.h` e084efc69b46 → 55c71414d9fc, regenerated; `DMSH_VK_ABI` stays 3d4e8bb1e1e8: old libraries keep playing while the delta runs). The hunk carries a `PEANUTS(upstream-candidate)` marker as well | Yes — a general fix: no game-specific branch; any dual-source shader on that driver |

`dm/gfx/tools/vk_android_delta.py` is untouched; it is an exception by the same rule if a future change
touches it.

The 2026-11-21 `vk_android_spv.py` edit's test, `test_dual_source_whole_store` and its `FS_DUAL` module in `dm/tools/tests/test_vk_android_abi.py`,
is marked `PEANUTS(upstream-candidate)` and goes upstream with it. No `vk_android_same.tsv` row: the library's bytes for a dual-source
shader change by design, so there is no same-bytes claim to record.

Since the update to Banana Native v0.3.26 (Round 17) `dm/gfx/nvn/dmsh_abi_vk.h` also differs from upstream's in one number: upstream's new kind
`DMSH_VK_KIND_IMG_UBO` is 25 here (23 upstream), because 23 and 24 are this fork's `TEX_DYN` and `SAMP_DYN` in every library its releases built.

## Generic host runtime-profile sites

Behind `-PnxGenericHost=true` only (`DM_RUNTIME_PROFILE`, defined by `dm/peanuts/profile/game_profile_generic.h`); the default per-title build and the
Mac/iOS Makefile build take the unchanged `#else` branch at every one of these, byte for byte. Listed here so a person rebasing a conflict at one of
these lines knows the `#ifdef DM_RUNTIME_PROFILE` branch needs the same fix, not just the `#else` one, and so a *new* `DM_GAME_*` macro's two other
required edits (a `gen_runtime_profile.py` table entry, and - only for a brace-initializer macro - a converted call site here) are not missed on the
next sync (found in review: upstream added 6 macros in the 73 commits since the fork point).

| File : site | Macro(s) | What the runtime branch does |
|---|---|---|
| `dm/gfx/nvn/hle_nvn.c` `nvn_api()` | `NVN_API_MAJOR`/`MINOR` | reads `g_profile.has_nvn_api` instead of `#ifdef` |
| `dm/host/hle_sdk16.c` `font_skip()` | `SHARED_FONT_TTF` | reads `g_profile.has_shared_font_ttf` instead of `#ifdef` |
| `dm/host/hle_sdk16.c` `h_oe_DispVer()` | `DISPLAY_VERSIONS` | reads `g_profile.display_versions[]`/`ndisplay_versions` instead of a `static const` array |
| `dm/host/hle_cheats.c` `profile_version()` | `DISPLAY_VERSIONS` | same table, second call site (the cheat panel's version line) |
| `dm/host/main.c` `host_preload()` module list | `MODULES` | reads `g_profile.modules[]` instead of `static const char *def[]` |
| `dm/host/main.c` `host_preload()` top | - | calls `profile_rt_load()`/`profile_rt_load_seed()`, before any `DM_GAME_*` read in this function |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_shader.c` `one_lane_forced()` | `SUBGROUP_ONE_LANE` | reads `g_profile.subgroup_one_lane[]` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_draw.c` `vegetation_cut()` | `MESH_VEGETATION_CUT` | reads `g_profile.mesh_vegetation_cut[]` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_draw.c` `prepass_listed()`/`mesh_cut()`/`prepass_draw()` | `MESH_PREPASS_CUT` | each reads `g_profile.mesh_prepass_cut[i][0..3]` (same `uint64_t[4]` row shape) |
| `dm/host/loader.c` `window_apply()` | `WINDOW_FN` | reads `g_profile.window_fn[]` |
| `dm/host/loader.c` `levels_apply()` | `RES_LEVELS` | reads `g_profile.res_levels[]` |
| `dm/host/probe.c` `drs_install()` | `DRS_CTL` | reads `g_profile.drs_ctl[]` |
| `dm/host/hle_fpslock.c` `k_steps`/`NSTEPS` | `FPS_STEPS`/`NFPS_STEPS` | `NSTEPS` becomes the profile's fixed row capacity (it also sizes other file-scope arrays); `k_steps` is a lazily-filled, `pthread_once`-guarded copy of `g_profile.fps_steps[]` |
| `dm/host/hle_render.c` `k_rcfg`/`k_rq` | `RENDER_CFG`/`NRENDER_CFG`, `RENDER_QUALITY`, `RENDER_DEFAULT_OUT` | `k_rcfg` is `dm_pf_rcfg_rows()` (loader.c), `k_rq` is `g_profile.render_quality`; the count and default output are plain scalars |
| `dm/host/loader.c` `dm_pf_rcfg_rows()` (new) | `RENDER_CFG` | builds `rcfg_row_t` rows (pointers into `g_profile.render_cfg[].build`) once, `pthread_once`; used by `render_cfg_row()`, `rt_pool_apply()` and hle_render.c |
| `dm/host/loader.c` `render_cfg_row()`, `rt_pool_apply()` | `RENDER_CFG`/`NRENDER_CFG` | read `dm_pf_rcfg_rows()` instead of a `static const` array |
| `dm/host/loader.c` `dm_pf_rtpool_rows()` (new), `rt_pool_apply()` | `RT_POOL`/`NRT_POOL` | builds `rtpool_row_t` rows over `g_profile.rt_pool[]` once, `pthread_once` |
| (no site edit) | `EVENT_WAIT`, `OCCLUSION_SPLIT_PASS`, `LOW_RAM_PLACE_PART_MB`, `LOW_RAM_IMG_CACHE_MAX_MB`, `LOW_RAM_APP_POOL_MB` | scalars read at run time (vka_worker.c, vka_exec.c, vka_tex.c, hle_svc.c); `game_profile_generic.h` maps each to its `g_profile` field |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_pso.c` | seed (`vk_prewarm_seed.h`) | not edited: `dm/peanuts/profile/vk_prewarm_seed_generic.h` defines the same `dm_vk_prewarm_seed`/`_len` symbols, set by `profile_rt_load_seed()` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_replay.c` `nxhost_replay()` top | - | calls `profile_rt_load()`/`profile_rt_load_seed()` too (nxreplay needs a profile for its `DM_GAME_ID` capture check) |

Also gated, but not `#ifdef`-paired with upstream code (new Peanuts-owned files/functions, not a duplicated site):
`android/host/CMakeLists.txt` (`nxhost_add_generic()`), `android/app/src/main/cpp/CMakeLists.txt` (one `nxlaunch` target), `android/app/build.gradle`
(`nxGenericHost` property, `nxGenericProfiles` task), `android/app/src/main/java/dev/nxnative/android/Native.java` (`load()`),
`android/app/src/ondevice/java/dev/nxnative/android/PackService.java` (`DM_UE_HOST_LIB`, `DM_PROFILE_PATH`/`DM_PROFILE_SEED` for the Oodle worker).

## Summary of good upstream-contribution candidates (general fixes, not Peanuts-specific)

1. `dm/host/plat/plat_android.c` — stack guard-page exclusion (IL2CPP GC crash fix)
2. `dm/host/crash.c` — stripped-library lib+offset in crash output
3. `dm/host/loader.c` — module arena (fixes an Android-only OOB read vs the console's layout)
4. `dm/host/hle_audren.c` — clock-paced render thread (fixes AAudio catch-up/audio drag)
5. `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_tex.c` — NULL-texture-registration clear, sampler descriptor table sized
   for every pool's span, buffer textures as 2D row-wrapped images
6. `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_exec.c` — sRGB↔linear converting copy between formats
7. `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_draw.c` — indirect quad/line-loop draws read on the CPU instead of skipped
8. `dm/gfx/tools/shader_split.py` — on-device worker-pool split + comment-insensitive table compare
9. `dm/gfx/tools/uepak.py` — off-Mac AES path (`_nxaes`) for the packer
10. `dm/gfx/tools/vk_android_build.py` (exception, not edited directly, but referenced wiring in
    `PackService.java`) — worker-pool/zstd-level changes, per review
11. `scripts/pack_android.py`: `split_shaders` and `swap_in` move a listing's files by rename; on an exFAT/FAT drive
    macOS moves each file's AppleDouble `._<name>` with it, so the listing's `._` entry is gone by its turn and the pack
    stops with FileNotFoundError (found packing Link's Awakening from a .nup on an exFAT drive). Both skip an entry that is
    gone. The same file's `peanuts_env` (the phone packer's DM_SPLIT_DIRECT=1 and DMSH_VK_ZSTD_LEVEL=6 for a computer's
    pack) is Peanuts-specific and stays local
12. Windows paths in the survey and the Unreal split: `dm/games/profile_guess.py` `romfs_files` (`as_posix()`), `dm/gfx/tools/uepak.py`
    `Toc` file names and `dm/gfx/tools/shader_split.py`'s two pak labels (`posixpath.normpath`). On Windows `os.path.normpath` / `str(Path)` gave `\`,
    so the provisional profile's `romfs_marker` read `TetrisEffect\Content\…` and the shader map's sources `..\..\..\Engine\…`
    instead of `Engine/…` (found on Tetris Effect: Connected, `dm/games/0100DE501433A000/README.md`). No change on macOS or Linux
13. `dm/tools/docs_patches.py` writes `docs/agents/game-patches.md` with `newline='\n'`: on Windows `Path.write_text` translated every line end to CR LF,
    so a regeneration showed the whole page changed. No change on macOS or Linux

`dm/host/hle_fpslock.c`'s new `EXTRA_VSYNCS_U8` lock kind is also upstream-worthy infra; its two
per-game rows (Link's Awakening, Echoes of Wisdom) should instead move to a Peanuts-owned
`dm/games/<id>/peanuts.tsv` overlay once that mechanism lands, so upstream's own `k_locks[]` table
stops needing a local edit at all.

## Round 6 (2026-11-14) — code mods whose modules branch into each other

A mod made for the console may branch straight from one module into another (Luminescent Platinum for
Pokemon Brilliant Diamond 1.3.0: 106 `b`/`bl` patched into main that go to the `subsdk1` it adds, and 1,228
from that module into main), at the distances the modules have on the console. The packer works those out
(`dm/tools/xmod.py`, new), the rewriter checks and lists them, the loader aims them again. Upstream hunks,
all behind "a build without code mods takes none of this":

| File | Hunk | Marker |
|---|---|---|
| `dm/tools/patch_nso.py` | `--xmod <layout file>` parsed with the other arguments; `xmod_table()` before the final summary line writes `<module>.xmod.tsv` or refuses the build. Without `--xmod` the output is byte for byte what it was (main and subsdk0 of Pokemon Brilliant Diamond 1.3.0 rewritten before and after: 16 of 16 output files the same, `build_src.sha256` apart, which records the rewriter's own hash), so the file before the change is kept as `dm/tools/rewriter_equiv/patch_nso.py.125819eecaa9` and existing packs stay up to date | a `PEANUTS:` comment and a `PEANUTS begin`/`PEANUTS end` block |
| `dm/host/loader.c` | `xmod_apply()`/`xmod_apply_all()` and its one call in `load_modules()`, after every module is mapped and before relocation: each row's `imm26` set for the bases the modules got here | `PEANUTS begin`/`PEANUTS end` block, a `PEANUTS:` comment on the call |
| `scripts/pack_android.py` | `cross_module()`, `xmod_keys()`, the `xmod` argument of `rewrite()`/`upgrade_keys()` (a module rewritten with a layout is keyed with it), an added module placed in the console's order (`merge_mod_modules`), `mod_build` rows in `pack.tsv` for the modules a mod supplies (a `build` row is compared with the game's own file, `BuildCheck.build`) | inside the existing `PEANUTS begin`/`PEANUTS end` blocks, `PEANUTS` comments on the single lines |
| `android/app/src/ondevice/java/dev/nxnative/android/PackService.java` | the "pack" case appends `Job.modArgs()`'s tokens too: `pack.tsv` listed the game's own modules and the host never loaded the added one | `PEANUTS begin`/`PEANUTS end` block |

Second pass, same day: the addresses such a module keeps as data. The mod's subsdk1 has 116 relative
relocations whose addend lies outside the module (its GOT entries for globals and functions of main, at the
console's distance); the first one read stopped the game at subsdk1+0x172b8. `patch_nso.py`'s `xmod_table()`
lists them as `ptr` rows (the same block; the file before is `rewriter_equiv/patch_nso.py.e9e2bc834168`, the
output without `--xmod` again byte for byte the same: main and subsdk0, 16 of 16 files), `loader.c`'s
`xmod_apply()` stores the real address after `relocate()` (the same block, a second marked call),
`pack_android.py`'s `cross_module()` reads them with `xmod.far_pointers()` (inside its block; `import struct`
marked).

No upstream-candidate general fix here either. `dm/tools/ips.py`, `dm/tools/patch_ips.py` (a patch's offsets
count the NSO's 0x100-byte header, as Atmosphère applies them; a patch file named by the whole build ID),
`dm/tools/xmod.py` and `dev.peanuts.build.ModBuild`/`ModdedBuild`/`Prepare` are Peanuts-owned files.

## Round 7 (2026-11-14) — haptics and motion inside a game's page

The launcher's haptics and motion (`dev.peanuts.ui.Haptics` / `Motion`, the vocabulary of `design/haptics-motion-proposal.md`) reach the page a
held cover opens, which upstream draws. One hook per widget kind, each a call into `dev.peanuts.ui.Feel` (Peanuts-owned, all the logic); no call
site of a row, a switch or a dialog is edited. Twelve hunks in four files, every one additive: a listener runs exactly as it did, with a haptic
before it; with haptics off and motion reduced (Settings › Library) or in another process of the app (the game, the readiness check) the hooks do
nothing.

| File | Hunks | Hook | Marker |
|---|---|---|---|
| `android/app/src/main/java/dev/nxnative/android/Ui.java` | 5 | `Ui.Setting`'s constructor (`Feel.row`: a row's tap), `Ui.Setting.toggle` (`Feel.toggled`: a switch), `Ui.button` (`Feel.button`), `Ui.Segmented` (`Feel.picked`), `Ui.navItem` (`Feel.nav`: a rail entry, and its pane's entrance) | trailing `// PEANUTS: ...` on each line |
| `android/app/src/main/java/dev/nxnative/android/PadFocus.java` | 2 | `Builder.create()` (`Feel.dialog`: a choice picked in the list), a `setPositiveButton` override (`Feel.confirm`: a confirm button with a listener) | trailing `// PEANUTS: ...`; a `PEANUTS begin`/`PEANUTS end` span |
| `android/app/src/main/java/dev/nxnative/android/MainActivity.java` | 3 | `gameSettings()` (`Feel.page`: the page enters), `gameSettingsTop()` (`Feel.tab` around `build()`: a tab's tap and L1 / R1), `gameTab()`'s game-files list (`Feel.added`: the rows read off the main thread) | trailing `// PEANUTS: ...` on each line |
| `android/app/src/main/java/dev/nxnative/android/GameSettings.java` | 2 | the header's Play (`Feel.launch`), `radio()` (`Feel.picked`: its dialog is a plain `AlertDialog.Builder`) | trailing `// PEANUTS: ...` on each line |

`Ui.java` and `PadFocus.java` are shared with upstream's other screens, so those get the same feedback from the same hooks (Home's cards and
Settings: rows, switches, buttons, choice dialogs, confirms); `dev.peanuts.ui.PUi` stays the copy without them (Peanuts' own screens call
`Haptics` themselves). No upstream-candidate general fix: every line calls Peanuts-owned code. The list of what plays where:
`android/app/src/main/java/dev/peanuts/settings/HOOKS.md`, section 4.

### Round 8, after the first device run (2026-11-15): queued region writes and forced image drops

Breath of the Wild at 1.5x aborted after 131 s in `img_destroy` (a second free). The cache gives an image up whatever its reference count in two places
(eviction, `vka_tex_prune_locked` / `img_evict_locked`, and `vka_tex_drop_pool`), and a queued region write (`mtl_tex_write_region`: WriteTexels) holds its
image by pointer with a reference only. An image the game writes but has not drawn for two frames can be evicted and freed at once with writes still queued;
the next prepare then copies into, and releases, whatever has taken the freed `vka_img`'s memory. With the resolution scale that can be the old image of an
in-place swap (it was made right after a prune, with a count of 1), which the stray release destroys once more than its own callback. Without the scale the
same stray release lands on a new image of the cache. This is the cause as far as reading the code and a model of it go
(`dm/host/tests/t_android_imglife.c`, `make android-imglife`; `build/t_android_imglife old` shows the fault); it has not been confirmed on the device.

| File | Hunks | Change | Marker |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka.h` | 1 | `vka_img.rw_pend`: region writes queued for the image | `// PEANUTS: ...` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_tex.c` | 7 | `rw_cancel_locked` and its forward declaration; `mtl_tex_write_region` counts a queued write (and sends a write to a scaled image through the whole upload); the drain skips a cancelled entry and counts down; `vka_tex_prune_locked` does not evict an image with queued writes; `vka_tex_drop_pool` takes them back first | `// PEANUTS: ...` on or above each line |
| `dm/host/tests/Makefile` | (same span) | `t_android_imglife`, `android-imglife` | inside the `# PEANUTS begin`/`# PEANUTS end` span of Round 8 |

Upstream candidate: yes, the `rw_pend` part (eviction and pool drops against queued region writes) is a general fix, independent of the resolution scale.

### Round 10, pacing on the panel's own refresh (2026-11-15)

`dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_panel.h` (Peanuts-owned, test `dm/host/tests/t_android_panel.c`, `make android-panel`): the panel's refresh period and phase estimated from the
actual present times `VK_GOOGLE_display_timing` reports. Two switches, both default on: `DM_VK_PACE_PANEL` (the host's pacing clocks tick in whole measured refreshes
instead of a free-running 60.000 Hz) and `DM_VK_PRESENT_GRID` (every game frame gets a desired present time on the measured grid). Frame generation's own timing is
as it was.

### Round 10, texel buffers the GPU writes (2026-11-15)

`dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_tbuf.h` (Peanuts-owned, test `dm/host/tests/t_android_tbuf.c`, `make android-tbuf`): how a read-only buffer texture bound to a texel buffer slot
is read. The copy of the CPU's bytes taken at recording (`DM_VK_TBUF_COPY`, our own earlier addition) is wrong for a buffer the GPU writes earlier in the same work.
Final Fantasy Resonance on an AYN Thor: the particle instance counts, written by compute dispatches through storage texel buffers, were read from leftover memory in a
scene's first frame, the indirect draws got a huge instance count and the GPU timed out in 40-50 % of the loads. Stores through a storage texel buffer are now kept as
GPU writes of that range, and while one is not done a read-only texel buffer over it is viewed in place, or for a dispatch copied on the GPU when the device cannot view
it there. `DM_VK_TBUF_GW=0` is the behaviour before. Buffers the GPU has not written keep the CPU's copy.

| File | Hunks | Change | Marker |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_tex.c` | 6 | the include; `gw_add` keeps the storage texel buffer flag when the same opaque range is recorded again; `vka_tex_gw_tbuf` (the flagged opaque write) and `vka_tex_gw_tbuf_pending` (such a write over a range is not done); `g_gw_tb` counts the flagged writes kept (raised in `gw_add`, lowered where a done write is dropped), so `vka_tex_gw_tbuf_pending` returns at once when there is none | `// PEANUTS ...`, `// PEANUTS begin`/`end` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka.h` | 1 | the two declarations | `// PEANUTS ...` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_draw.c` | 5 | the include; `tbuf_gcopy_end` and `tbuf_gw_note` (the `tbuf gw` log line, once per shader, binding and path); `tbuf_view`: the choice through `vka_tbuf_path`, the copy on the GPU (`vkCmdCopyBuffer` into scratch memory after a full barrier), the pending dispatch barrier recorded before a dispatch that reads such a buffer in place; `tbuf_view`: a writable storage texel buffer marks its range; `vka_dispatch`: the barrier after the copies, before the dispatch | `// PEANUTS ...`, `// PEANUTS begin`/`end` |
| `dm/host/tests/Makefile` | 1 | `android-tbuf` | `# PEANUTS begin`/`end` |
| `docs/env_switches.tsv` | 1 row | `DM_VK_TBUF_GW` | generated table, row by hand |

Left as it was: a draw's read-only texel buffer that the GPU wrote and the device cannot view in place (no copy can be recorded inside the draw's pass: the CPU's copy,
with a `tbuf gw` line saying so); ranges written through storage buffers, raw global memory or texture to buffer copies (their marks can cover far more than was
written, so they do not take the CPU's copy away); buffer textures read as 2D images (`DM_VK_TBUF`, `vka_tex.c`), which are uploaded again once a GPU write is done.

### Round 10, draws that are not skipped for a background pipeline (2026-11-15)

`dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_psosync.h` (Peanuts-owned, test `dm/host/tests/t_android_psosync.c`, `make android-psosync`): with `DM_VK_PSO_ASYNC` on (the launcher's default) a draw
whose pipeline the cache lacks is skipped until a background thread has made it. Super Mario Odyssey on an AYN Thor drew a black picture with only the HUD for a whole
first run (no pipeline cache yet), and still did with every pipeline made: a skipped draw's result was kept by the game. One fixed rule, with no switch, on whenever
`DM_VK_PSO_ASYNC` is: two kinds of draw get a missing pipeline on the frame path instead, as with `DM_VK_PSO_ASYNC=0`: a draw into a target of at most 64x64 of the
game's pixels, and a draw that writes memory or runs with the rasterizer off. Verified on the AYN Thor (Odyssey's first launch is right with the first kind alone).
Two more kinds were tried the same day and taken out with their switches: draws of at most 6 vertices (a 2D game draws everything that way: OCTOPATH TRAVELER 0
had 99 pipelines held on the frame, frames up to 564 ms) and draws into targets not drawn every frame (no game needed it; 36 more held there).

### Round 10, texture copies go by the textures' formats (2026-11-15)

Super Mario Odyssey on an AYN Thor (Adreno 740, Turnip; the same picture on an Adreno 840 with Qualcomm's driver, issue #21): water, crystal and other refracting materials
drew flat bright green. `copy_t2t` chose between a plain copy and a converting blit by the two images' formats; a copy between two R11G11B10 textures, the second sharing
an R32 float texture's image (learned view formats), was converted into the float image and read back as R11G11B10. The copy now goes by the textures' formats, and a copy
that does convert with such a side goes through scratch images of the textures' formats. Verified on the device with learned view formats on. A profile key that turned
learned view formats off for the game was written first and taken out again: the profile's format is as it was.

| `docs/reference/rendering.md`, `docs/agents/symptoms.md` | 1 entry, generated | "Refracting materials flat green through a shared image of two formats (Android)" | `PEANUTS` in the Symptom text (the index copies it) |

### Round 10, Import again, and a build that asks before it goes on without DLC shaders (2026-11-15)

Three gaps the round above left ("Round 10, missing shaders that point at DLC that was not imported"):

1. The build pressed Try anyway for the player on every failure that offers it, so the new message was never seen and the game was built without the
   shaders. `dev.peanuts.build.Prepare` now stops for one cause, add-on content that was not imported, and the build card offers "Import again with DLC" (or
   "Set the DLC folder") and "Try anyway". The other failures that offer Try anyway are tried as before (`dev.peanuts.build.AutoRelax`).
2. Nothing imported a game a second time: Build and Rebuild use the `.nup` that is there. The game sheet has "Import again…" for a game whose base is a `.nup`
   (`dev.peanuts.build.Reimport`, `GameSheet.importAgain`): it names what is missing and does nothing, or asks and then makes the `.nup` anew from the dumps
   found now. The new file is written beside the old one, which is replaced only when the import is complete (`BuildService.stepImport`); deleting the old file
   first is a second choice when both do not fit. The control interface has the same as `reimport` (`peanuts/docs/CONTROL.md`).

   own beside `pack.tsv` (`peanuts-pack.tsv`, `dev.peanuts.build.PackNote`; `pack.tsv` and its format are not changed, and a pack without the file reads as
   before): `stale` is written by the import before a new `.nup` takes an old one's place, and `Prepare` then leaves the old job and makes the shaders and the
   pack again; `without` says how many shaders a Try anyway build left out for add-on content, and the sheet shows it.

### Round 10, the shaders of another listed version (2026-11-15)

A correction first. Commit 1d034f07's message, and the two rounds above, say Mario Kart 8 Deluxe lacked 135 of 29,785 shaders because it was imported without
its Booster Course Pass. That was wrong. The profile's `game.tsv` has `shader_versions v1441792 v1507328`: its tables are the union of updates 4.0.0 and 4.0.1,
and the 135 are 4.0.1's own (the 135 rows of `shader_map.tsv` whose source starts with `/v1507328/`). The game's add-on content holds only `contents.dat` and
`version.txt`; the courses are in the update. The dump was 4.0.0 alone, so nothing was missing from it. The example file named in that round's test
(`/Course/B3ds_WuhuTown/course.ptcl`) was made up, not seen.

`dm/games/README.md` gives the rule for `shader_versions`: a version is built from the profile as it is only when all listed versions are there, else from a
derived profile with that version's own tables. Only `scripts/build_ipa_from_nsp.py` did that. The pack now does the smallest equivalent, on the PC and the phone
alike: when the dump's version is one of several listed, the map's rows tagged with the other listed versions (`/v<N>/…`, `main@v<N>`, as `shader_split.py`
writes a shader that first appears in a later version) are keys the dump may lack, exactly as the keys of `shader_optional.tsv` are. The build goes on with no
failure and no Try anyway, says one line (`N of M shaders in this app's table belong to other versions of the game (vB): not needed for vA`), and the pack is not
marked as built without shaders. Downstream needed nothing: the phone's shaders step already builds from a key list without the absent keys with
`DMSH_VK_REQUIRE_ALL=1`, and the PC's already leaves the keys of `_absent.tsv` out. No shader-chain file is changed.

The bound is exact, not a guess: only tagged rows of other listed versions may be lacking (135 for Mario Kart 8 Deluxe 4.0.0), and any other missing key is
refused as before. What the tags cannot say is which untagged rows a later version dropped, so a dump of 4.0.1 alone that lacks 4.0.0-only shaders is still
refused; `shader_optional.tsv` is the way to name those (Super Mario Bros. Wonder's names both sides: 3,457 of 1.2.1 only, which are exactly its tagged rows, and
3,451 of 1.0.0 only). A dump whose version is not in `shader_versions`, and a profile with one version, behave as before.

The add-on content message of the earlier round stays, but only for a profile whose tables are of one version, and after this check. No game is known that needs
it, and it is untested against a real one. Import again (the round above) stands on its own: a newer update or added DLC still needs it.

Peanuts-owned, no upstream file: `dm/peanuts/rt/shim/pnrt_services.h`, `shim_readback.cpp`, `shim_services.cpp` (the runtime's side), `dm/peanuts/rt/peanuts_patches.{h,cpp}`
(manifest patches), `dm/peanuts/peanuts_services.{c,h}` (the host's side of battery, frame totals and add-on content, and the `Bridge.battery` entry; the header
holds the parts that only compute), `dm/peanuts/rt/test/svc_test.cpp` and `vka_readback_test.c`, and the test targets of `dm/peanuts/rt/CMakeLists.txt`
(`svc_test`, `gaps_test`, `vka_readback_test`, run by `ctest`).

Texture readback. A module asks for one texture's level 0 as R8 or as four bytes a pixel, by the address of its storage or as the newest of a size, optionally only
colour targets, only sRGB or linear ones, only ones drawn into in two frames. The request is only queued; the present thread picks the image under the texture cache's
lock with the runtime's own rule, holds it, records one `vkCmdCopyImageToBuffer` into the present's command buffer (the method of `DM_VK_IMG_DUMP`'s diagnostic) and
hands the bytes to a worker thread when the present has next waited for that submission's fence (three presents later; no fence is queried, since a status query
blocks on the drivers `vka_dev.c` probes for). The present's command buffer is queued ahead of the batch still being recorded, so each image carries the fence of the
batch that last wrote it: a copy reports the newest generation whose batch is on the queue, and an image made or first written in a batch not yet queued waits for
the next present. A request no present takes within two seconds is answered as failed by the next request, so a paused game never leaves the two slots taken.
Formats are the emulator's: plain 8-bit R8, RGBA8 and BGRA8, linear or sRGB, the stored bytes; nothing compressed, float, packed or multisampled. The limits are
the runtime's (2048 a side, 16 MiB, two requests a second, two at once), so at most 32 MiB a second is copied. With no request queued the present pays one relaxed
load and a branch; a GPU write's note, a new image's first use and a colour target's site pay one each, false until a companion's first request. A rescaled image
(`DM_RES_SCALE`) is not read. A request's address is the texture's storage address in the game's memory: this host has no GPU address space.

The smaller services. `sysfont:` serves the firmware fonts the importer wrote as `.ttf` (`DM_SHARED_FONT`, `DM_SHARED_FONT_EXT`, `cjk_*.ttf` beside the standard
font) under the firmware's file names, in the stored form the lifted source decrypts; never a file of `DM_FONT_DIR`, none over 64 MiB. `aoc:` is the lowest index
of `DM_AOC_DIR` (a folder, a `.nup` or a dump; when that index cannot be read, nothing). The battery is what the app pushes through `Bridge.battery`, else the
kernel's power supply files. `@perf.fps` counts the replay's frames; `@perf.frametime_ms` is the GPU's busy time a frame (the emulator's is a frame's time without
its waits). A manifest's patches are written with the game's threads stopped and no thread inside them, only where main holds the file's own instructions and what
it held before the package's load plan was installed; a patch that cannot be written just now is tried again on later ticks; the pages keep their protection.

The shims and stubs that call these functions are files of other work in progress; `dm/peanuts/rt/PORT.md` says what is true once their lines are in. Checked on a
PC: `vka_readback_test` (the replay's `.inc` compiled against a stub Vulkan table: 53 checks over submit, record, the wait for a batch, hand-over, timeout and every
failure), `svc_test` (135 checks: the lifted broker over a fake replay, the six firmware fonts byte for byte, add-on content, host values, patches, and the same
through `Tegra::GPU`, `PerfStats`, `GetPowerStatus`, `OpenAocRomfs` and a package with patches loaded by `pn_rt_create`), My Time at Sandrock's package in `rt_test`
with a fake texture source, its live-fog code against the broker, and the NDK compiler's syntax check of every new and changed file. No APK was built and nothing was
run on a device. No shader-chain file is changed (`dmsh_chain.py` `FILES` and `vk_android_build.py` `VK_FILES` name none of the replay's files). Upstream candidate:
no (upstream has no companion runtime).

### Round 11, three faults in the host's waits, and what a thread dump says (2026-11-16)

Found while looking for a lost wake-up behind a rare freeze of Echoes of Wisdom (none was found in these paths: the condition variable, mutex, event,
address and exit paths read as the kernel's and held through twenty minutes of a stress test with real threads, which does catch the old
"release the mutex, then write the key" order within a second).

| File | Hunks | Change | Marker |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dm/host/hle_thread.c` | 14 | (1) a native wait entered inside another counted down instead of up (the stack-address line of the round above had taken the `else`): the outer leave then stopped the process; (2) `thr_runnable` took a thread held by a stop of all threads for a blocked one, so the thread that woke a higher-priority peer on its core ran beside it after the stop; the game's own `SetThreadActivity` pause still counts as blocked; (3) on Android a guest wait's timeout is measured on the monotonic clock: on the wall clock, a clock set back during play left a timed wait asleep for that long; (4) `svc_thread_dump` adds three lines a thread: `wait:` (how long, the timeout, for `WaitSynchronization` each handle's kind and whether it is signalled, a pending cancel), `sched:` (runnable or why not, whose handoffs hold it, whom its own handoff waits for) and `history:` (its last eight synchronisation calls: age, call, two arguments, result, time blocked, who ended the wait, how many threads it woke) | `PEANUTS` in each hunk |
| `dm/host/tests/Makefile` | 1 | `peanuts-sync` (part of `peanuts-host`, 3 s) and `peanuts-sync-control` (the old order must fail) | inside the `PEANUTS begin`/`end` block |
| `dm/host/tests/t_peanuts_syncstress.c` (new) | whole file | the three faults (each fails on the code before), then a storm: pool waiters and signallers with the SDK's "signal only if the key is not 0", events signalled from a host thread and a game thread against waits with and without timeouts, cancels, address waits, threads made and ended, mixed core masks, optionally stops of all threads throughout | `PEANUTS` on its first line |

Run on a Linux PC only (also under TSan). The Mac's tests of the scheduler (`t_preempt`, `t_system_event`, `t_notify_sched`) were not run and should be,
since `thr_runnable` changed for threads held by a stop of all threads. No shader-chain file is changed. Upstream candidate: (3) yes; (4) the `wait:`
and `history:` lines yes; (1) and (2) no (they are in code only this fork has).

follows is what happened to the hunks of the rounds above, and the hunks the update added. A hunk not named here merged as it was.

A texel buffer binding takes the fork's view first and upstream's `vka_tbuf_view` where that has none and for a texture record; upstream's `DM_VK_TBUF` (0, 2) holds for both. `vka_dispatch` refreshes upstream's images, then binds inside the fork's `g_tbuf_disp` bracket |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_tex.c`, `vka_draw.c` | buffer textures as 2D images of rows | upstream has its own image of a buffer texture now (`vka_tbuf.c`: a copy the GPU makes of the guest memory when it runs, every frame, viewed in the texture's own format). The fork's row image stays the one a shader reads (made of the CPU's bytes when the draw is recorded, uploaded where the CPU wrote, viewed in the shader's numeric class), outside the pass as before: `vka_tex_id_pending` counts a buffer texture again. Upstream's copy is taken only where `tbuf_img_gpu` (vka_draw.c) says so: stores through a storage texel buffer over the range are in work that is not done, and the copy can be made and read (a shape and a format of the binding's class, placed memory). The first form of this merge took upstream's copy whenever it could be made, which read the game's next frame out of a buffer it had refilled (the race of `DM_VK_TBUF_COPY`'s note) and copied Three Houses' lists every frame; corrected in the same round. The answer is kept for the draw or dispatch (`vktb_memo`, `vka_tbuf.h`), since the tracker's answer changes when the draw's own earlier binding records a store, and the tracker reads the completed fence as the submission's prepare read it (`g_gw_done`, `vka_tex.c`), not as the fence watcher has raised it since; the copy is bound only when it was made, or found current, as the draw's images were made. A buffer texture bound as a storage image takes the row image with storage use, as before the merge (upstream binds nothing there): the stores stay in that image, which is said once |

## Xenoblade X dynamic frame rates (2026-11-17)

The Android launcher/host FPS foundation is reused from local Peanuts commit
`fb5ce6f7` (the unrelated optional resolution-code feature is excluded).
The 1.0.2 profile adapts FPSLocker Warehouse's exact-build timing corrections,
credited in its game note. Host memory rows add integer constants, reciprocal
clamped elapsed FPS and a 32-bit greater-than movie-state cap. Loader plans
check all expected words before mutation and use separate RX/RW auxiliary pages.
The profile stays experimental: title/movie checks do not validate gameplay.
See [the game note](../dm/games/0100453019AA8000/README.md#experimental-30--60--120-fps).

## Android presentation deadline forwarding

The active game Activity forwards `Display.getPresentationDeadlineNanos()` once during `hostSetup`, then through the existing 500 ms `fpsTick` while active. A missing display sends zero. The JNI bridge preserves positive nanosecond values as 64-bit unsigned values, normalizes nonpositive values to zero and safely ignores a host without the new weak interface. The renderer uses this platform queue-lead requirement when selecting its target presentation grid; half a refresh alone does not describe the compositor's deadline. See the [Android API contract](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/view/Display#getPresentationDeadlineNanos()).

There is no additional timer or persisted preference; the first tick after resuming a paused loop can take up to two seconds. Deadline-aware physical 120 Hz testing still showed uneven presentation despite sufficient reported queue lead. Forwarding the deadline corrects the source's platform contract but does not establish a cure for that platform behavior.

| Files | Marked hook | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `GameActivity.java` | `presentationDeadlineUpdate`, `hostSetup`, active `fpsTick` | Forward the current display's required queue lead |
| `Native.java`, `nxlaunch.c` | `presentationDeadlineSet`, weak forwarder | Preserve nanoseconds and handle missing hosts |
| `vk_android.h`, `vka_present.c` | `nxhost_presentation_deadline_set` | Expose and consume the platform's compositor deadline |

`peanuts/tests/test_presentation_deadline.py` compiles the production Activity forwarding helper against JVM stubs and the production JNI bridge against the exported C header. It checks deadline changes, missing displays, 64-bit values, signed-input normalization and unavailable weak host symbols.

### Stable 30 display-mode platform mitigation

For the owner's Stable 30 preference, the game Activity requests a same-resolution mode within 0.5 Hz of 60 Hz only when the fourth field of the running native cap status is 30 and requested native FG state is explicitly off. The third status field is the configured game rate, not an observed frame count; zero means unavailable state. Pending restart preferences never drive this request. Every other cap, unavailable state, FG enablement, pause/background transition and destruction releases it. No global setting is changed.

Final combined physical 60 Hz menu/outdoor/movement windows measured 630/630 uniform two-refresh intervals (378 while walking or moving the camera) while nominal 120 Hz retained 12–18 short/long pairs per 126 intervals despite the platform deadline lead. The remaining high-refresh compositor/HWC cause is open. This is an explicit platform mitigation for stable 30 fps, not a demonstrated root-cause cure; mode selection remains an asynchronous platform request.

| Files | Marked hook | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `GameActivity.java` | helper field, active `fpsTick`, pause and destruction | Apply and release the request before updating the display deadline |
| `Lsfg.java` | `push` call-out | Release the Activity request immediately before an in-app native FG enable call |
| `Native.java`, `nxlaunch.c` | `frameGenEnabled`, weak forwarder | An absent interface leaves display selection unrestricted |
| `vk_android.h`, `vka_framegen.c` | `nxhost_framegen_enabled` | Read initialized requested FG state independently of rendering warm-up |

The helpers live under `dev/peanuts/display`. External native cap/FG changes are observed by the active 500 ms tick, with up to two seconds for the first resumed tick. Physical display switching is asynchronous. `peanuts/tests/test_running_display.py` compiles the real policy/helper and in-app enable path against JVM stubs, and the production native getter/setter against C stubs. It checks effective-cap parsing, mode geometry/rate, cap/FG changes, missing state, idempotent window updates and request release before the native enable call.

### Pipeline cache save failure paths (2026-11-17)

`dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_pso.c` has PEANUTS(upstream-candidate) hunks closing cache, stamp and prewarm streams once, replacing files only after a complete successful close, removing failed temporary files and freeing failed-save blobs. `peanuts/tests/test_pipeline_cache_save.py` injects write, close, rename and open failures in the production writer under ASan/UBSan, including the actual prewarm save function and its dirty-state retry. This is an error-path correction, not an attribution of the observed judder; the measured runs show no cache-save I/O errors.

### Texture release scanned the whole serial map under the texture lock (2026-11-18)

`dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_smap.h` has PEANUTS(upstream-candidate) hunks: each image counts its entries in the serial map (`smap_n`, a PEANUTS field of `vka_img` in `vka.h`), kept by `vksm_put` and `vksm_del_ent`, so `vksm_del_im` returns at once for an image without entries and stops at its last one. Before, every image let go (`unref_locked`, from `nvnTextureFinalize`) walked the whole table while holding `g_tmu`. Measured on the Thor in Breath of the Wild (T30 driver, 30 s simpleperf profile with off-CPU time, frame cap 60, streaming terrain): 3.2 s of CPU in that walk on the game's threads, the replay thread 5.5 s waiting for the lock, 28 waits of 15-76 ms, frames of 50-140 ms with no pipeline or texture work on them. `dm/host/tests/t_android_smap.c` checks the counts against a reference and the empty case. Evidence: `pn-build/botw-userplay-20261117` (local).

### A diagnostic switch read from the environment at every draw (2026-11-18)

`dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_draw.c` `draw_body` has a PEANUTS(upstream-candidate) hunk: `DM_VK_BIND_LOG`'s keys are parsed when a switch changes (`vka_env_gen`, as `skip_fs` does) instead of `getenv` at every draw. In the same Thor profile of Breath of the Wild that `getenv` was 5.6 % of the replay thread's CPU (32 ms a second) with the switch unset. The switch stays live.

## Captain Tsubasa 2: World Fighters 1.0.6

Added the Peanuts profile and game index entry for `01009720213B0000`. The generated shader tables are listed in `upstream-unmarked.txt` so their bytes match the PC split. [Game note](../dm/games/01009720213B0000/README.md)

`dm/tools/ue_malloc.py` accepts a relocated primary vtable header when the exact Itanium type name matches FMallocBinned3. `dm/host/hle_uemalloc.c` carries that header into the replacement allocator. Both changes carry PEANUTS markers and apply to Unreal builds with RTTI. `dm/tools/tests/test_ue_malloc.py` covers the verified and mismatched type names.

`dm/host/hle_offline.c` adds the PEANUTS-marked SDK hook for the application server environment enum. [Offline NSD environment query](../docs/reference/services.md#nsd-application-server-environment-query-aborts-during-startup)

## Double Dragon Revive

The [profile](../dm/games/01006AE02236C000/README.md) registers the base and update executables with their decoded IoStore shader tables. `dm/tools/ue_region.py` recognizes their startup size store before an outlined reservation helper; `dm/tools/tests/test_ue_region.py` checks the matching global, ambiguity and the caller-only gate. Neither game build is marked Android-playable on host evidence.

## DAVE THE DIVER 1.0.6.1267

The Peanuts Android profile registers the updated executable, PC-generated shader tables and a Vulkan startup prewarm seed from Thor. The exact build uses the shared `step_ticks_u32` memory operation to scale Unity's fixed timestep to the 20–120 fps caps. The generated tables and binary seed are listed in `upstream-unmarked.txt`. [Game note](../dm/games/010097F018538000/README.md)

## Link's Awakening manual frame rate

`Profile.java`, `GameActivity.java` and `SidePanel.java` share the PEANUTS-marked engine-target policy for explicit frame rate modes. `ProfileOptionsJvmTest.java` covers native, static-plan and dynamic FPSLocker modes. [Game note](../dm/games/01006BB00C6F0000/README.md#android-frame-rate-choices)

## Kirby and the Forgotten Land frame rate

The 1.1.0 profile uses reviewed FPSLocker rows for caps from 20 to 120 fps. `build.gradle` and `fpslocker_rows.h` accept four offsets after the module-relative pointer. `test_fpslocker_rows.py` covers the chain and rejects a fifth offset. [Game note](../dm/games/01004D300C5AE000/README.md#android-frame-limits-from-20-to-120)

### Round 23, Swap screens (2026-11-18, GitHub issue #131)

Some dual-screen setups attach the top screen over USB-C, so Android lists the built-in bottom screen as the default display and the clip-on top screen as an
external one (the Retroid Pocket 5 with its Dual Screen Add-On): the game started on the bottom and the companion on the top. Settings › Library › Swap screens
(`PeanutsPrefs.swapScreens`, only shown with a second display) starts the game on the external display. The companion takes the display the game is not on, so
it also goes to the built-in one when the app itself was started on the external display, with the setting off.

| File | Hunks | Hook | Marker |
|---|---|---|---|
| `android/app/src/main/java/dev/nxnative/android/MainActivity.java` | 1 | `startWithDriver()`: `Screens.startGame(this, intent)` | trailing `// PEANUTS: ...` |
| `android/app/src/main/java/dev/nxnative/android/FrontendLaunch.java` | 1 | the game's start: the intent as a local `play`, `Screens.startGame(this, play)` | trailing `// PEANUTS: ...` |
| `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml` | 1 | `dev.peanuts.CompanionScreen`, process `:game`, its own task affinity, single instance | trailing `<!-- PEANUTS: ... -->` |

`Screens.startGame` checks whether Android allows the external display and retries on the current display if the external launch fails. Android refuses a `Presentation` on the default display, so the companion page (now `CompanionPage`, shared with
`CompanionPresentation`) gets an activity there, `CompanionScreen`. Its own window takes that display's focus but no touch, hides the system bars and takes Back
as the game's Back; the page is a panel above it that never takes the focus, so a tap leaves the controller with the game (#183, all in Peanuts' own files).
Its start still makes the built-in display the top one, which would hand it the controller, so `Companion.refocusGame` starts the game's activity again on its display (singleTask: brought to
the front; an intent with no title ID is a no-op in `GameActivity.onNewIntent`). No upstream-candidate general fix: the lines call Peanuts-owned code.

### Round 31, the game back on its own display (2026-11-20, GitHub issue #183)

A phone's Recents starts the task it is given on the display Recents runs on. With Swap screens on the Retroid Pocket 5, Recents on the bottom screen and
back into the game moved the game to the bottom and the companion to the top. Recents and Home also closed `CompanionScreen`, which came back only after
the game had been in the background. `GameActivity` now tells `Companion` each time it becomes the top activity again. `Companion.place` (rule in
`ScreenPlace`) sends the game back to the display it was started on when it has a task of its own (Swap screens), or lets the companion follow it (the game in Home's task, a refused move, a display that is
gone), and opens the companion again. The same round makes the built-in display stay on top on a phone without per-display focus, where Back from its
navigation bar otherwise found no focused window (`Companion.screenTouched`, all in Peanuts' own files).

### On-device packer archive (2026-11-19)

The marked `nxPackTools` exclusion in `android/app/build.gradle` omits developer notes and duplicate prewarm seeds from the phone packer archive. The host receives each seed through its generated runtime asset or compiled header.

The marked `packageRelease` rule requests a full packaging step for Peanuts builds. Incrementally replacing `tools.zip` left the previous 23 MB entry inside the signed APK.

`dm/tools/vk_prewarm.py` reads the declared number of records, including records flagged bad, before merging a capture into a seed. A truncated record header aborts the merge. The parser and its regression in `dm/tools/tests/test_vk_prewarm.py` carry upstream-candidate markers.

The environment and Advanced tables document `DMSH_VK_ABSENT_FILE` as an internal packer path. The troubleshooting wiki documents the build-log share action. The Linux fixtures in `test_vk_encoder_wait.py` and `test_romfs_image.py` now compile the current encoder worker and build their synthetic index validator under GCC warning checks.

### Digimon Story Time Stranger: MVGL archives, engine shaders, sampler getters, its frame rate and job pool (2026-11-20)

Digimon Story Time Stranger (`010062E01FE0C000`) keeps every shader in Media.Vision's MVGL archives, in a layout with 32-bit counts and 64-bit offsets (`app_0.nx64.mvgl` is 6.2 GB) whose compressed entries are LZ4 blocks; the split read that archive whole, which fails on macOS for one read of more than 2 GB (`OSError: [Errno 22]`), and found no shader. The engine's own shaders are the same NX?S containers inside `main`, which the control-section heuristic paired none of (black screen). It needs the sampler-builder getters of the section above (it crashed without them). Its FPSLocker Warehouse config needs rate offsets. Its animation job pool empties its job vectors under running workers (stops and crashes): a per-build cut.

### Targeted shader splits for released games (2026-11-19)

`dm/gfx/tools/shader_split.py` resolves a single registered build's paths in multi-version profiles and recognizes
file offsets in Diablo III's CPK sources. A registered executable outside the profile's explicit shader versions triggers a full scan.
Single-build validation checks shader keys, package groups, facts and pairs. Profiles without optional or version-specific keys skip a second parse of the shader map.
The generated `shader_files.tsv` tables for Wonder, both Kirby games, Pokémon Sword and Pokémon Shield include files
that contribute pairs without introducing new shader keys. The targeted packages for those games and Diablo III
matched their full-scan references by SHA-256. The Python hunks and profile tables carry `PEANUTS` markers.
### Round 20, textures placed without a join (2026-11-18)

A texture in a shader-code pool placed its parts and queued a join like a buffer, so one segment of a large pool placed by parts was joined whole again by every texture across its end (Kirby and the Forgotten Land: 32 to 352 MiB, 17 to 84 ms a join, then refused past `DM_VK_PLACE_JOIN_MB`). Nothing reads a texture's storage in one piece; the uses that do ask for themselves. Not a shader-cache input. Reference: [Joins for textures grow one segment until a join is refused](../docs/reference/memory.md#joins-for-textures-grow-one-segment-until-a-join-is-refused-android).

| File | Hunks | Change | Marker |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_mem.c` | 5 | `mtl_pool_place`: `mtl_pool_need`'s placement with no join queued and none reported (`pool_need(…, join)`). The per-thread record of asks with nothing left to do (`negent`) notes whether its ask joined, so a placing ask's record does not answer a joining ask of the same range (found in review of the pull request: a linear or buffer texture, a texture's address or a command naming it, asked on the same thread after the texture's placing ask, returned from the record and its two parts were never joined) | `PEANUTS(upstream-candidate)` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_place.h` | 1 | `vkp_neg_hit`: when a record answers an ask | `PEANUTS(upstream-candidate)` |
| `dm/gfx/nvn/hle_nvn.c` | 1 | a texture in a shader-code pool asks with `mtl_pool_place` | `PEANUTS(upstream-candidate)` |
| `dm/gfx/nvn/nvn_mtl.h`, `dm/gfx/nvn/nvn_mtl.m` | 1 each | the declaration; Metal's no-op | `PEANUTS(upstream-candidate)` |
| `dm/host/tests/t_android_place.c` | 2 | the model of Kirby's pool: joined for textures a segment grows past 256 MiB, placed only it stays one part; the record's cases | `PEANUTS(upstream-candidate)` |

### Android build retries and shader completion reports

`Job.java` saves the PID of the running pack service. `PackService.java` and the game exit notice share
`ExitWatch.exitInfo` to find the first exit for that PID and process name since the recorded run. Older jobs
without a PID keep their worker counts. `PrepareText.java` points an interrupted build at the log for its cause.
The service drains the tool log before the final job save and scans each chunk once. A JVM replay with two pack
processes selected the interrupted job’s native crash. A 2,304,000-byte backlog retained the final shader summary.

### Round 30, an upsample pass reads the level its sampler pins (2026-11-19, GitHub issue #170)

Breath of the Wild's Sheikah glow (the pedestal in the Shrine of Resurrection) came out low-resolution and blocky on the Thor. `view_of` gave every mip-chain pass a view of the levels below its attachment, which assumes the pass downsamples; an upsample pass renders level n and reads level n + 1 through a sampler pinned to it, and Vulkan clamped that read to level n - 1. The rule of nxnative's `fb_pinned_level` (its pull request 1073, MIT; MacNativeGames took it for Tears of the Kingdom) on the Android replay: a sampler that pins one level samples that level alone, any other keeps the levels below. A general fix, to go upstream. Runtime only: the shader chain does not move. Reference: [Upsample pass reading the level above the one it renders (Android)](../docs/reference/rendering.md#upsample-pass-reading-the-level-above-the-one-it-renders-android).

| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_draw.c` | 1 | a sampled texture binding's view asked with its sampler ID (`vka_tex_id_view_samp_rec`), a storage image's without | `PEANUTS(upstream-candidate)` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka.h` | 1 | the declaration | `PEANUTS(upstream-candidate)` |

### Round 58, the build queue (2026-11-22, issue #348)

A build asked while another runs waits its turn instead of being refused: `dev.peanuts.build.BuildQueue` keeps the games in order in the app's own storage
(`files/build-queue.tsv`), and `BuildService` (now sticky) builds one after the other, also after Android ended the app's process. A game that fails or is
stopped is marked and the next one starts. Before each game the queue waits while upstream's `PackPolicy` says so (heat, battery; read by reflection, with the packer's
remembered Go on anyway, #93), while a game of the app runs and while a build with mods runs; a `.nup` that does not fit the free space, or less
than 0.5 GB where the pack is made, stops that game before anything is written. The library shows the queue (the covers' "Queued, 3 of 5", the Building
filter's Up next card, Remove, Stop all, Resume after a pause, Dismiss on a stopped card), the notification the place ("2 of 5"), Cancel all, and one alert
at the queue's end. Build all… queues every game that needs a build (Build needed, stopped, or built by an earlier version of the app). `Prepare` waits while
the packer still runs another job, and takes the packer's messages only of its own job. Upstream's own Prepare goes into the queue while it runs:

### Round 29, compressed game files: .nsz and .xcz (2026-11-19, issue #149)

The importer builds a game from NSP and XCI files compressed with nsz: an `.ncz` entry is an NCA stored as zstd with the keys and counters of its
encrypted ranges, and `dm/peanuts/import/nx_ncz.c` gives the NCA reader its original bytes through a store below it (`nx_nca_open_store`), so the `.nup` is
the uncompressed files' byte for byte. A block NCZ is read in place, a solid one unpacked once into a temporary file beside the `.nup`; each is checked
against its name and its CNMT record before the writer starts. Stop reaches the importer (`nximport_cancel`, `Native.cancel`), also for a plain NSP. The
app lists the files and counts their real size and the temporary space (`dev.peanuts.build.Compressed`, `LibraryScanner`, `GameSheet`, `BuildService`);
the desktop builder takes them (`peanuts_build.py`, `nximport_cli check`). zstd is the vendored decoder (`dm/third_party/zstd`, unchanged), compiled into
`libnximport` from the importer's own CMakeLists. The run-time reader (`peanuts_nsp.c`) still refuses compressed dumps. Docs: `dm/peanuts/import/HOOKS.md`
"Compressed dumps", `peanuts/docs/FAQ.md` "Can I build from NSZ or XCZ files?", `peanuts/docs/BUILDER.md` "Compressed files".

|---|---|---|---|
| `README.md` | 1 | "your own game dump" names NSZ and XCZ | `PEANUTS` (an HTML comment at the line's end) |
| `.github/workflows/peanuts.yml` | 1 | a step that builds the importer and runs its ctest (`nsp_test`, `font_test`, `ncz_test`, ...), which no workflow ran before | `PEANUTS` (the comment above the step) |

### Game owned by default for a profile (2026-11-20, GitHub issue #153)

`fps_owned_default 1` in a game's profile starts it Game owned until the player picks a frame rate, while the limits from `fps_limit` and `fps_limit_max` stay offered. Kirby and the Forgotten Land uses it: its elapsed-time plan collapsed to 4 fps in the overworld on a Snapdragon 8+ Gen 1, where the game's own pacing held 30.

### First launch: shader parts for the game's depth mode, retyped compute in the prewarm list (2026-11-21)

A first start prepared every program's shader parts for near = -w, the default before a run's stamp says otherwise; a game in near = 0 threw them all away at its first program and compiled them again while it played (Hyrule Warriors: Age of Calamity: 3,441 programs prepared in 77 s for nothing). 18 of the 24 seeds are near = 0. The depth mode now comes from the profile's seed (its graphics keys' `gl_depth`), `DM_VK_PSO_PREP_DEPTH0=0/1` overriding. A compute program bound to integer images runs as a retyped variant (`umask`, `smask`), built on the frame path and never written to the prewarm list, so no seed made it ahead (Age of Calamity: ~10 s of frozen frames at its first battle); it is now a compute record with `klen` 16 holding the two masks, which the prewarm builds. An older host skips such a record (`vka_pw_rec_ok`).

| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_pwlist.h` | 1 | a compute record is `klen` 0 or 16 | `PEANUTS` |
| `dm/host/tests/t_android_pwlist.c` | 1 | the 16-byte compute record accepted, 8 refused | `PEANUTS` |

### Yoshi's Crafted World fixes 1/9: integer buffer-texture classes before a draw's images (2026-11-22, GitHub PR #140, 999sian)

A draw prepared its buffer textures (`make_images`) before it knew which of them the shader reads as integer textures (`tex_masks`), and bound them after. A buffer texture a compute pass had just written could then be prepared under one dimension key and bound under another, which bound an outdated CPU copy instead of the GPU-written rows: Yoshi's Crafted World's Rail-Yard Run hung on a OnePlus Pad 2 (Adreno 750, Turnip T30) and reached its results screen with the fix.

| File | Hunks | Change | Marker |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_draw.c` | 2 | `draw_body`: the pipeline key and `tex_masks` before `make_images` | `PEANUTS` |

### Yoshi's Crafted World fixes 2/9: image retirement covers the recording submission (2026-11-22, GitHub PR #140, 999sian)

An image whose last reference went during a submission's texture prepare (a queued region copy, an upload) was destroyed after the fence of its last draw, which could be older than the command buffer being recorded. The replay now keeps the highest fence whose recording has begun (`g_recorded_fence`, set at `vka_tex_prepare` from the worker's reserved fence or, inline, the next fence) and retires images no earlier than it.

| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_exec.c` | 2 | the submission's fence passed to the texture prepare | `PEANUTS` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_worker.c` | 3 | the reserved fence (0 inline) passed to `vka_rp_sub_begin` | `PEANUTS` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_tex.c` | 5 | `g_recorded_fence`; `unref_locked` and `vka_lrz_touch` retire through `vto_retire_fence` | `PEANUTS` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_texown.h` | 1 | `vto_retire_fence` | `PEANUTS` |

### Yoshi's Crafted World fixes 3/9: bounded index and draw ranges (2026-11-22, GitHub PR #140, 999sian)

Index buffers are bound with their real byte range where the device has `maintenance5` (core in Vulkan 1.4, else `VK_KHR_maintenance5`; the instance now asks for 1.4 where the loader has it): `vkCmdBindIndexBuffer2KHR` through the encoder, the legacy bind elsewhere. A direct draw needs its whole index range placed; an indirect one binds every whole mapped index. Indirect-count multi-draws of quads and line loops, which are expanded on the CPU per record, no longer take the native multi-draw path. Expanded index counts are checked before any allocation, a zero-size vertex binding reads zeros, vertex-state ranges are checked without overflow, and the instanced-run fast path checks the memory generation, the device limits and zero-stride streams. The transient blocks carry index and vertex buffer usage.

|---|---|---|---|

| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_dev.c` | 6 | instance API 1.4 where available; `maintenance5` (core or extension) enabled and `vkCmdBindIndexBuffer2` resolved; index and vertex usage on transient blocks | `PEANUTS` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_draw.c` | 24 | `ib_bind` with a size; bounded direct and indirect index ranges; expanded-draw guard; `vka_mdi_gpu` refuses expanded topologies; `ub_hazard` ranges; `vin_bind` size 0; instanced-run guards | `PEANUTS` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_draw_range.h` | new | `vkdi_index_span`, `vkdi_arg_span`, `vkdi_expanded_count` | `PEANUTS` |

### Yoshi's Crafted World fixes 4/9: a direct sampler chosen by the view's format (2026-11-22, GitHub PR #140, 999sian)

A direct combined descriptor pairs the guest's sampler with one image view. Where that view's format has no `FILTER_LINEAR` (or `FILTER_MINMAX` for a min/max reduction), linear filtering is invalid, so that pairing gets a nearest-only variant of the guest sampler (same wrap, LOD range, bias and border); every other pairing keeps the guest's filters. The format is the one the view was really made in, after class retypes and format-list fallback (`image_view`'s `actual`, kept in the view memo). The guest sampler cache was capped by the bindless sampler table's size, which is 0 before that table is made or where it is not made; it is now capped by `maxSamplerAllocationCount`. The Vulkan self-test samples its checkerboard linearly and checks an edge texel.

| File | Hunks | Change | Marker |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka.h` | 2 | `vka_tbuf_image_view`, `vka_tex_id_view_rec`, `vka_tex_id_view_samp_rec` report the view format; `vka_samp_id_rec(id, view_fmt)` replaces `vka_samp_id` | `PEANUTS` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_draw.c` | 7 | `stage_writes` passes the view's format to the sampler; a placeholder view takes the default sampler | `PEANUTS` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_tbuf.c` | 1 | the row image's view format | `PEANUTS` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_tex.c` | 16 | `image_view` with `strict` and `actual`; the view memo keeps the format; `vka_samp_id_rec` with per-ID filtered and nearest variants; `sampler_of` capped by `maxSamplerAllocationCount` | `PEANUTS` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_sampfilter.h` | new | `vksf_required`, `vksf_nearest` | `PEANUTS` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_selftest.c` | 5 | linear checkerboard sampler, a direct sampler before any submission, the edge texel read back | `PEANUTS` |
| `dm/host/tests/t_android_sampfilter.c` | new | the filter requirements | `PEANUTS` |
| `dm/host/tests/Makefile` | 2 | `t_android_sampfilter` built and run | `PEANUTS` |

### Yoshi's Crafted World fixes 5/9: streamed mips mirrored into guest storage (2026-11-22, GitHub PR #140, 999sian)

Unreal grows a streamed texture by copying its resident levels into a new texture on the GPU, then writing the finer levels from the CPU. The first copy made the whole destination GPU-owned, so the CPU's later writes were dropped (Yoshi's Crafted World 1.0.1: 53 block-compressed images with mip 0 all zero). A plain whole-level copy between compatible, CPU-backed 2D textures now also copies that level in guest storage (`nvn_tex_mirror_t2t`) and the destination keeps following CPU writes; any other GPU write keeps the old ownership rule. The Vulkan self-test reads back a copied level and a later unflushed CPU fill of the finer one.

| File | Hunks | Change | Marker |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka.h` | 1 | `vka_tex_mirror_mip` | `PEANUTS(upstream-candidate)` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_exec.c` | 1 | `copy_t2t` mirrors a plain image copy | `PEANUTS(upstream-candidate)` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_tex.c` | 2 | `mirror_mip_storage`, `vka_tex_mirror_mip`; the region-write queue declared before them | `PEANUTS(upstream-candidate)` |
| `dm/gfx/vk_android/vka_selftest.c` | 2 | `streamed_mip_test` | `PEANUTS(upstream-candidate)` |

### Round 52, two threads of one core in a font engine (2026-11-21, Tomodachi Life: Living the Dream's boot crash)

Tomodachi Life: Living the Dream 1.0.4 crashed about 3 s into a boot in the SDK's font engine (`FSS_get_advance`, `map_char`; reads at 0x20 to 0x28), in 3 of 8 boots with one save on the AYN Thor (8 of 15 in an earlier series) and 1 of 3 in PR #86; with the fix in none of 24 (16 on Turnip, 8 on Qualcomm's driver). NintendoWare Font, linked into the game, keeps one `nn::fontll::ScalableFontEngine` per core and picks it by the caller's core number; the engine takes no lock. The job worker `AppJobThreadPool0` (priority 44) and the `Scene Prepare Thread` (50) are both pinned to core 0: on Horizon they never run at once, on the host they did, and one thread's `SetFont` (`FSS_set_font` clears the state's font pointers before it sets them again) ran while the other was reading them. The host now gives each engine the exclusion a Horizon core gives it: a call never runs beside another, and between two threads pinned to the same core the engine stays with the one that used it last until that one blocks (its next SVC, a native HLE wait, its exit), unless the other has the higher priority, which takes it between two calls.

| `dm/host/hle_thread.c` | 4 | `dm_core_excl_held`, `dm_core_excl_release` and `dm_thread_id_self`; the release at `thr_svc_enter` and at a native wait's entry (`dm_thread_native_wait`); `dm_thread_core_excl_wait` (same core, owner runnable, same or higher priority) and `dm_thread_describe` for the log | `PEANUTS` (begin/end spans and line comments) |
| `dm/host/hle.h` | 1 | the declarations | `PEANUTS begin`/`end` |
| `dm/host/hle_sdk16.c` | 3 | `Initialize` and `LoadFont` take-overs enter and leave the engine through hle_fontll.c's pointers (NULL in a test of hle_sdk16.c alone) | `PEANUTS` |
| `dm/host/tests/Makefile` | 1 | `peanuts-fontll` (part of `peanuts-host`) | inside the `PEANUTS begin`/`end` block |
| `docs/reference/threads.md` | 1 | the entry "Two threads of one core in a font engine" | `PEANUTS` (HTML comments) |
| `dm/games/010051F0207B2000/README.md` | 2 | the PR #86 crash resolved; "The boot crash in the font engine" and "Found on this game" | `PEANUTS` |

A build with exclusion per call only fails 3 of them (the higher-priority thread measured 779 sequences with the other's font; an owner that does not block, and one that exits, were not waited for). Upstream candidate: yes (the Mac host runs a core's threads side by side too).

### Level-5 VFS3 shader objects (2026-11-22, GitHub PR #230, tapionallanaire)

`shader_split.py` reads Level-5's VFS3 archives (version 16, generation 1: Ni no Kuni II's `data/preload_zstd.vfs`), which index names and zstd block lists by file ID and store compressed members as blocks rather than whole frames. `vfs3_members` rebuilds each shader member's frame (with the archive's dictionary where it names one) and checks every range and size; each member's stage must match its extension. On Ni no Kuni II 1.0.0 the split reads 902 distinct shader keys; the game has no profile. `shader_split.py` is not a shader-cache input (`dmsh_chain.py` `FILES`), so no library version moves.

### Linux shader tools (2026-11-21)

The packer's shader tools now run on Linux: `dm/gfx/tools/tool_lock.py` guards the macOS-only `xcrun` Metal queries behind `sys.platform == 'darwin'` (Linux has no Metal toolchain), and `dm/gfx/tools/vk_android_build.py` starts the translator through `tool_lock.xlate_command` (on Linux, dotnet runs the portable `dmsh_xlate.dll`; the apphost in the repository is a Mach-O). The shader chain procedure of `peanuts/docs/UPSTREAM.md` §3.3 was run for the `vk_android_build.py` edit. The lock and the chain headers are left to the maintainer, who regenerates them during integration (CONTRIBUTING.md).

1. `tool_lock.py --update-lock` on Linux drops only the two `metal_*` rows; the translator dlls, `xlate_source` and `spirv-cross` (the in-repo Linux build in `spirv_cross_bin/`) are unchanged. The committed lock is the upstream one (with the metal rows), so the tools digest is the same on Linux and macOS.
2. With the upstream lock, the tools digest is `cc4a8ae11c32`; the new `vk_android_same.tsv` row resolves it to `e084efc69b46`, the version upstream's tree expects and the phones carry, so no library version moves.
3. `DMSH_VK_ABI` `3d4e8bb1e1e8` unchanged: the moved chain is a translate pass, not an outage (no full rebuild of installed devices).
4. Byte-compare: a full Mario Kart 8 Deluxe build on Linux (all 29,785 `shader_map.tsv` keys, the 134 the package lacks left out, `DMSH_VK_REQUIRE_ALL=0`, 29,651 shaders) is byte-identical to the same full build with upstream's own tools on the Mac: both files 204,778,511 bytes, SHA-256 `7f1bc569e684efab467d000acb6bee68e89d00cf8ebd30a8644081e25869e336`. The Mac build ran in a worktree at upstream `main` `606320f` with the committed Mach-O apphost; the Linux build ran in this tree with dotnet starting the portable `dmsh_xlate.dll`. Row appended to `dm/gfx/tools/vk_android_same.tsv` (`cc4a8ae11c32 → e084efc69b46`).
5. `vk_android_build.py --check` of the rebuilt library: every module decompresses and passes spirv-val --target-env vulkan1.3.

### New Pokemon Snap: DRPF reader (2026-11-21)

The DRPF reader (`dm/gfx/tools/drp.py`) and its hook in `shader_split.py` read New Pokemon Snap's 1,034 `.drp` archives: each entry decrypted in one XorShift stream under the seed it carries, its Oodle payload decoded by the caller's decoder (the Mac host's or the ooz worker, `DM_OODLE_OOZ`) and checked against the seed, then scanned like a loose file (the shaders are GLSLC outputs in the SSBH shader databases of its `.nushdb` entries). The split reads 3,316 shaders from the dump (8 compute, 2 geometry, 1,743 pixel, 1,563 vertex) where main's 8 GLSLC outputs were all it found before. `shader_split.py` is not a shader-cache input (`dmsh_chain.py` `FILES`), so no library version moves. Provenance: the format and crypto are a port of kwsch/NewSnap's DRPF implementation (`NewSnap.Lib/DRPF/{DrpArchive,DrpFileHeader,DrpFileEntry}.cs` and `NewSnap.Lib/Encryption/XorShift.cs`, ISC licence, credited in drp.py's header), not original Peanuts work, so an upstream PR carries NewSnap's ISC notice; the GPL-3.0-licensed finalpantasy/new-pokemon-snap-recomp census, which describes the same layout, was read for understanding and nothing was copied from it. Test: `peanuts/tests/test_drp.py` (the reader's round trip and the split's DRPF branch, no game data).

|---|---|---|---|

### Level-5 NNDXDHSC shader objects (2026-11-22, GitHub PR #243, tapionallanaire)

`shader_split.py` `nndx_shader` reads Level-5's `NNDXDHSC` version 1 objects (Inazuma Eleven: Victory Road's base CPK members under `data/nx/shader/`), one stage each: the stage tag at `+0x0c` (vertex 1, pixel 2, geometry 3, compute 6), the code and control ranges at `+0x48` / `+0x50`, both checked, and a graphics stage checked against its SPH header. `scan_blob` takes a blob by this magic, not by extension, and the records (`#NNDX`) take the single-stage pairing path. The game has no profile. `shader_split.py` is not a shader-cache input, so no library version moves.

| File | Hunks | Change | Marker |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dm/gfx/tools/shader_split.py` | 3 | `nndx_shader`, its `scan_blob` branch, and `#NNDX` in `SINGLE_STAGE` | `PEANUTS(upstream-candidate)`, `PEANUTS` |
| `docs/reference/shaders.md` | 1 | Level-5 NNDXDHSC shader objects | `PEANUTS(upstream-candidate)` |
| `docs/agents/symptoms.md` | 1 | the reference entry's symptom line (`docs_symptoms.py`, allow-listed) | none |
| `dm/tools/tests/test_split_nndx.py` (new) | whole file | the four stages from the header, and refused ranges and stage disagreements | `PEANUTS` in its header |

The game reads the pool builder's device, memory, size and flags back through four getters the host did not answer; the calls returned zero and the game's resource pipeline read a null pool. The getters return the builder's fields. A general fix: any NVN title that reads a builder back needs them.
